Test pattern: `Test-Case "name" { ... Assert <bool> "message" }`. ASCII only, PowerShell 5.1, no network.

### [x] T27.5 - upgrade-project.ps1: an appended .gitignore entry is never glued onto an unterminated last line   (Story S27)
- **Goal:** when upgrade-project appends entries to an EXISTING `.gitignore` whose last line has no final newline, the first appended entry starts on its own line: last line `custom/` + appended `.env` stays two lines, never `custom/.env`. An empty file, or one that already ends in a newline, gets no extra separator. The user's existing bytes are never rewritten. The entries, their order, the conditions and the console lines stay the same.
- **Touches:** `upgrade-project.ps1` - ONLY step 2 "Git safety net" (the `if (Get-Command git ...)` block, ~lines 145-172 as of this map; locate by content): the two `[System.IO.File]::AppendAllText($gi, ...)` calls in its `else` branch (the `$missing` append and the "bin/`nobj/`ndocs/.index/`n" append), plus one small helper defined inside that block. `test-kit.ps1` - one new `Test-Case` directly AFTER T27.4's case `"S27 follow-up: .gitignore keeps its line ending"` and before `"dad-doctor's fix hints name commands that actually fix the thing"` (anchor by name). Do NOT touch: the S26 guard (stays first); `Get-ProjectEol` / `ConvertTo-Eol` (reuse unchanged); the `$gEol = Get-ProjectEol $gi` line; the fresh-file `WriteAllText`; the `$secretIgnores`, `$noiseIgnores2` and `$noiseIgnores` arrays (the line `$noiseIgnores  = @(".claude/")` is pinned by a source regex in the T10.5 case); the `$cur`/`$missing` computation; both `if` conditions; both `Write-Host` lines; the git-untrack and git-init steps; the existing S26/S27 cases (T27.4's included); `new-project.ps1`, templates, `docs/`.
- **Do:**
  1. Helper. Define it inside the step-2 block directly after `$gEol = Get-ProjectEol $gi`. The script runs top-down, so it must be defined before the `else` branch calls it:
     `function Get-GitignoreLead([string]$Path, [string]$Eol) { $raw = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($Path); if ($raw.Length -gt 0 -and $raw[$raw.Length - 1] -ne [char]10) { return $Eol }; return "" }`
     It returns one line break in the file's own ending when the file is non-empty and its last character is not "`n", and "" otherwise. Compare the last [char] as shown. Do not use `$raw.EndsWith("`n")`: it is culture-sensitive, and under .NET 5+ ICU "`r`n".EndsWith("`n") is false.
  2. At EACH of the two appends, compute the lead right before the call, from the file as it is at that moment, and prepend it to the appended text:
     - `$lead = Get-GitignoreLead $gi $gEol`, then `[System.IO.File]::AppendAllText($gi, ($lead + (ConvertTo-Eol (($missing -join "`n") + "`n") $gEol)), (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)))`;
     - `$lead = Get-GitignoreLead $gi $gEol`, then `[System.IO.File]::AppendAllText($gi, ($lead + (ConvertTo-Eol "bin/`nobj/`ndocs/.index/`n" $gEol)), (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)))`.
     Never compute the lead once and reuse it. After the first append the file ends in a newline, so the second append must get NO separator (a reused lead inserts a blank line before `bin/`). When `$missing` is empty, only the second append runs, and it needs its own check.
  3. Keep everything else as it is: the entries, their order, the final terminator, both conditions, both console lines, UTF-8 no BOM, and `$gEol` as the ending. Append only; never rewrite the user's file. Optionally add one comment line under the existing `# The .gitignore writes follow the S27 rule ...` comment: an append onto an unterminated last line starts with one `$gEol` (T27.5).
  4. Test-Case `"S27 follow-up: an appended .gitignore entry is never glued onto an unterminated last line"`, directly after T27.4's case.
     - Before the case's own `try`: `if (-not (Get-Command git -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) { Skip-Case "git not on PATH - upgrade-project only writes .gitignore when git is present" }` (the same line as T27.4's case; `$haveGit` is defined later in the file, so do not use it).
     - Neighbouring S27 style: `$sb = New-Sandbox`; `try { ... } finally { Remove-Sandbox $sb }`; `$up = Join-Path $kit "upgrade-project.ps1"`; `$claudeLf = ($script:S27Lines -join "`n") + "`n"`. Each sandbox is a subfolder of `$sb` made with `New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force`. It gets `Write-S27File "<dir>\CLAUDE.md" $claudeLf` (without a CLAUDE.md, upgrade-project exits 1), and its `.gitignore` is written with `Write-S27File` (exact bytes). Run each as `$o = (& powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $up -ProjectDir <dir> 2>&1 | Out-String); $code = $LASTEXITCODE`, then `Assert ($code -eq 0) "(x) upgrade-project failed: exit $code. Output: $o"`. Read the result with `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText` (never `Get-Content`). `-ProjectDir` is always a folder under `$sb`, never `$kit`.
     - Fixtures. None has `.env` or `bin/`, so both appends fire, and none has a `.gitattributes`:
       - (a) `$pa = Join-Path $sb "lf-unterminated"`, `.gitignore` = "node_modules/`ncustom/" (no final newline; it holds "`n", so its ending is LF);
       - (b) `$pb = Join-Path $sb "crlf-unterminated"`, `.gitignore` = "node_modules/`r`ncustom/" (no final newline; its ending is CRLF);
       - (c) `$pc = Join-Path $sb "lf-terminated"`, `.gitignore` = `$origC = "node_modules/`ncustom/`n"` (already ends in a newline).
     - Run all three first, reading `$rawA`, `$rawB` and `$rawC`. THEN assert, with the glue check first. Assert throws at the first failure, so this order lets one message name BOTH sub-cases:
       `$glued = @(); if ($rawA.Contains("custom/.env")) { $glued += "(a) LF" }; if ($rawB.Contains("custom/.env")) { $glued += "(b) CRLF" }`
       `Assert ($glued.Count -eq 0) "appended entry glued onto an unterminated last line ('custom/.env') in: $($glued -join ', ')"`
     - (a): `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("$pa\.gitignore") -notcontains 13`; `$rawA -match '(?m)^custom/$'` and `$rawA -match '(?m)^\.env$'`; `$rawA.StartsWith("node_modules/`ncustom/`n.env`n")` (`.env` is the first `$secretIgnores` entry, so it is the first one appended); `-not $rawA.Contains("`n`n")` (no blank line anywhere, which catches a lead reused for the `bin/` append); `$rawA -match '(?m)^bin/$'`.
     - (b): `Test-S27Crlf "$pb\.gitignore"` (CRLF throughout, no bare LF); `$rawB.StartsWith("node_modules/`r`ncustom/`r`n.env`r`n")`; `-not $rawB.Contains("`r`n`r`n")`.
     - (c): `$rawC.StartsWith($origC)`; `$rawC.Substring($origC.Length).StartsWith(".env")` (the byte right after the original content is the first entry's first char: no separator, no blank line); `-not $rawC.Contains("`n`n")`.
  5. Mutation check. It edits the kit repo's `upgrade-project.ps1` TEMPORARILY; test-kit still runs upgrade-project only against `%TEMP%` sandboxes:
     - First, stage the finished T27.5 edit with `git add -- upgrade-project.ps1`, so the index holds the intended version.
     - Remove the leading separator (e.g. make `Get-GitignoreLead` always `return ""`). Run `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\test-kit.ps1 -SkipBuild` and confirm the new case FAILS with the glue message naming BOTH "(a) LF" and "(b) CRLF".
     - Restore with `git checkout -- upgrade-project.ps1` (from the index) and confirm `git diff -- upgrade-project.ps1` prints nothing (`git diff --quiet -- upgrade-project.ps1` exits 0). Finally, `git diff --cached -- upgrade-project.ps1` must show hunks ONLY in the step-2 `.gitignore` block.
  6. Run `dotnet build local-tools\local-tools.csproj -c Release`, then the full `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\test-kit.ps1`.
- **Acceptance:** the full `.\test-kit.ps1` prints `0 failed`; `"S27 follow-up: an appended .gitignore entry is never glued onto an unterminated last line"` appears on a PASS line (not SKIP); these also stay on PASS lines: `"T10.5 (C4b): non-DAD dir gets no .claude; .claude occupied by a FILE -> exit code unchanged; .claude/ is gitignored by new-project and upgrade-project"`, `"scaffold and upgrade ignore .claude/ (agent worktrees are not source)"`, the three S26 cases, the three S27 AC cases, T27.3's case and T27.4's case `"S27 follow-up: .gitignore keeps its line ending"`; mutation: removing the leading separator makes the new case FAIL with the glue message naming both (a) and (b) - verify, then restore, and `git diff -- upgrade-project.ps1` (working tree vs the staged T27.5 edit) is empty.
- **Depends on:** T27.4 (both edit step 2 of `upgrade-project.ps1` and `test-kit.ps1`, and T27.5's case is anchored on T27.4's; sequenced last in the Build order)
- **Refs:** Story S27 (Goal - "an upgrade changes only the content it means to change"; `docs/DESIGN.md` R15); defect confirmed by dev-agent in a %TEMP% sandbox both before and after T27.4, human-approved as a cleanup task 2026-10-03; T27.4 Context ("Known edge, out of scope: a `.gitignore` with no final newline gets the first appended entry glued to its last line") - this task is that fix; T27.4 Do step 3 (the two `AppendAllText` calls); CLAUDE.md "Secrets" (real values live in a gitignored `.env`); upgrade-project.ps1 step 2 "Git safety net" (`$gi`, `$gEol`, `$secretIgnores`, `$missing`, the two `AppendAllText` calls), `function Get-ProjectEol`, `function ConvertTo-Eol`; test-kit.ps1 `$script:S27Lines`, `function Write-S27File`, `function Test-S27Crlf`, `function Skip-Case`, T27.4's case `"S27 follow-up: .gitignore keeps its line ending"`, the T10.5 case (source regex on `$noiseIgnores = @(".claude/")`) and `"scaffold and upgrade ignore .claude/ (agent worktrees are not source)"` (source regex on `.claude/`).
- **Context:** defect: `AppendAllText` writes the block right after the file's last byte. When the last line has no final newline, the first appended entry is glued onto it (`custom/` + `.env` -> `custom/.env`). That breaks the user's rule, and `.env` is NOT ignored; the kit's secrets rules depend on it being ignored. Confirmed in a %TEMP% sandbox both before and after T27.4. `$gEol` (from `Get-ProjectEol`, computed before anything writes `$gi`) is the existing file's own ending when it holds any "`n" (CRLF if it holds any "`r`n", else LF). A file with no "`n" at all (e.g. a single unterminated line) falls to the `.gitattributes` rule, else LF, so it gets that ending as its separator. An empty file gets no lead, and the appends start at byte 0. A missing file takes the fresh-file branch and never reaches the appends. The step only runs when `git` is on PATH. This task does NOT reopen Story S27 (see the Build order note): S27 stays `DONE`, and when T27.5 is ticked close-unit finds S27 already DONE and changes nothing in STORIES.md. This task edits `upgrade-project.ps1`, so QA runs upgrade-project ONLY on sandboxes under %TEMP% - never against the kit repo (D:\projects\DrDad) or D:\Tools. `test-kit.ps1` tests the repo copy through `$kit`; the installed copy only picks the change up on the next `install.cmd`, which is not part of this task. Test pattern: `Test-Case "name" { ... Assert <bool> "message" }` (`Test-Case` itself sets `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"`; `Assert` throws at the first failure). `Get-Content` strips line endings - never use it for the ending checks. ASCII only, PowerShell 5.1, no network.

### [x] T27.6 - S27 cleanup: test that mixed endings, a no-newline CLAUDE.md and an empty .gitignore follow the stated rules (test-only)   (Story S27)
- **Goal:** `test-kit.ps1` pins the three S27 Behavior rules that have no test yet. (a) An existing CLAUDE.md that MIXES endings is rewritten uniformly CRLF (any CRLF wins). (b) An existing CLAUDE.md with no newline at all follows the fresh-file rule: `.gitattributes` `eol`, else LF. (c) An EMPTY existing `.gitignore` gets no separator before the first appended entry. A regression in any of the three then FAILS instead of passing silently. Test-only: no behaviour change.

Reuse `$script:S27Lines`, `Write-S27File`, `Test-S27Crlf` and `Skip-Case` unchanged. Do NOT touch: the existing S26/S27 cases (T27.3's, T27.4's and T27.5's included) or the S27 helpers; `upgrade-project.ps1` (edited TEMPORARILY for the mutation checks only, and restored exactly); `new-project.ps1`, templates, `docs/` (STORIES.md, DESIGN.md).
- **Do:**
  1. Case `"S27 follow-up: mixed endings, a no-newline file and an empty .gitignore follow the rule"`. Optionally put one comment line above it (`# T27.6: ...`), like the neighbours.
     - First line of the body, BEFORE the case's own `try`: `if (-not (Get-Command git -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) { Skip-Case "git not on PATH - upgrade-project only writes .gitignore when git is present" }`. This is the same line as T27.4's and T27.5's cases. `$haveGit` is defined later in the file, so do not use it.
     - Then use the neighbouring S27 style: `$sb = New-Sandbox`; `try { ... } finally { Remove-Sandbox $sb }`; `$up = Join-Path $kit "upgrade-project.ps1"`; `$claudeLf = ($script:S27Lines -join "`n") + "`n"`.
     - Each sandbox is a subfolder of `$sb`, made with `New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force <dir> | Out-Null`. Write every fixture with `Write-S27File`, so the bytes are exactly the ones the test chose.

     - Read results with `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText` / `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes`, never `Get-Content`. `-ProjectDir` is always a folder under `$sb`, never `$kit`.

  2. (a) Mixed CLAUDE.md -> uniformly CRLF. `$pa = Join-Path $sb "mixed"`, no `.gitattributes`.
     - `CLAUDE.md` = `$script:S27Lines[0] + "`r`n" + (($script:S27Lines[1..4]) -join "`n") + "`n"`, i.e. "# Project: t`r`n## Stack`n- x`n## Modes`n- old modes text`n": exactly one CRLF line, the rest bare LF.
     - Fixture guard: `$fx = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText("$pa\CLAUDE.md")`; `Assert (([regex]::Matches($fx, "`r`n").Count -eq 1) -and ($fx -match "(?<!`r)`n")) "fixture error: the mixed CLAUDE.md must hold exactly one CRLF and some bare LF"`.
     - Run, then assert: `Test-S27Crlf "$pa\CLAUDE.md"` (every line ends CRLF, no bare LF; message e.g. "(a) a mixed CLAUDE.md (one CRLF line, the rest LF) is not CRLF throughout after upgrade - any CRLF must win"); the raw text no longer contains `- old modes text` (it really was rewritten); the raw text contains "## Stack`r`n- x`r`n" (the user's section is kept, now CRLF).
  3. (b) CLAUDE.md with no newline at all -> the fresh-file rule. There are two sandboxes, and in both `CLAUDE.md` = "# Project: t" (one line: no "`n", no "`r").
     - Fixture guard in each: `$fb = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes(<its CLAUDE.md>)`; `Assert (($fb -notcontains 10) -and ($fb -notcontains 13)) "fixture error: the no-newline CLAUDE.md holds a line break"`.
     - This single line is the file under test AND the CLAUDE.md upgrade-project needs (it exits 1 without one). It has no kit-owned section, so the refresh still runs and APPENDS every kit-owned section from the template (`## Modes / flow ...`, `## Secrets ...`, ...). The result is multi-line, and that is what makes the ending check meaningful.
     - (b1) `$pb1 = Join-Path $sb "nonl-lf"`, no `.gitattributes`. Run, then assert: `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes("$pb1\CLAUDE.md") -notcontains 13` (message e.g. "(b1) a no-newline CLAUDE.md with no .gitattributes has CR bytes after upgrade - the fresh-file rule is LF"); the raw text `.StartsWith("# Project: t`n")`; the raw text matches `'(?m)^## Modes'` (the kit sections were added).
     - (b2) `$pb2 = Join-Path $sb "nonl-crlf"`, `.gitattributes` = "* text eol=crlf`n". Run, then assert: `Test-S27Crlf "$pb2\CLAUDE.md"` (message e.g. "(b2) a no-newline CLAUDE.md under eol=crlf is not CRLF throughout - the fresh-file rule was not used"); the raw text `.StartsWith("# Project: t`r`n")`; the raw text matches `'(?m)^## Modes'`.

Other cases may also fail under a mutation; what is required is the new case's FAIL with the named sub-case.

- **Refs:** Story S27 Behavior (amended 2026-10-03) in docs/STORIES.md - the bullets "A file that already mixes LF and CRLF is treated as CRLF when it contains any CRLF (human decision 2026-10-03, rule kept). A rewritten file comes out uniformly CRLF", "An existing file with no newline at all is handled by the fresh-file rule (`.gitattributes` `eol`, else LF)" and "A file that already ends with a newline, or an empty one, gets no separator"; grades/S27_GRADE.md iteration 2, suggestion 4 ("It is still untested; see 5") and suggestion 5 [dev] (human-approved as this cleanup task 2026-10-03). - **Context:**
  - `Get-ProjectEol` reads an EXISTING file as raw text. If the text holds any "`n", it returns "`r`n" when the text contains "`r`n" (so a mixed file is CRLF), else "`n". A missing file, or one with no "`n" at all (an empty file included), falls to the fresh-file rule. That rule is the `.gitattributes` subset: patterns `*`, the exact file name, `*.<ext>`; `eol=lf|crlf`; last applying line wins; default LF. `* text eol=crlf` applies to CLAUDE.md through the `*` pattern.
  - Step 3 reads CLAUDE.md with `Get-Content`, which splits on both CRLF and LF, so a mixed file's lines come out clean. It splits the lines into sections at `## ` headers, replaces kit-owned sections (`## Modes`, `## Secrets`, ...) from templates\generic\CLAUDE.md and appends the kit sections the project lacks. It writes `($out -join $cmEol)` trimmed plus `$cmEol`: one uniform ending. A single-line "# Project: t" therefore comes out as "# Project: t", an empty line, then "## Modes / flow ..." and the other kit sections.
  - Step 2 runs only when git is on PATH. An existing `.gitignore` takes the append branch. For an empty file, `Get-Content` yields nothing, so every entry is "missing"; `$gEol` is LF (fresh-file rule, no `.gitattributes`); and `Get-GitignoreLead` returns "" because the file has length 0. The `$missing` append therefore starts at byte 0 with `.env`. The `bin/` append re-computes its lead, which is "" because the file now ends in "`n". The writes are UTF-8 without BOM.
  - Without git, the whole case SKIPs, (a)/(b) included. `Skip-Case` must be called before the case's own `try` (S21 rule), and it aborts the case, exactly as in T27.4's and T27.5's cases. On this machine git is present, and the Acceptance requires PASS, not SKIP.
  - upgrade-project exits 1 without a CLAUDE.md, so every sandbox has one.
  - Its git step runs `git init`, `core.autocrlf false`, `git add -A` and a baseline commit in the sandbox. Under `eol=crlf`, git may print LF/CRLF notices on stderr. They land in `$o` and the exit code stays 0, so assert exit 0 and never assert that git printed nothing.
  - In .NET regex `(?m)`, `$` does not match before "`r". So for CRLF text, check line content with `StartsWith` / `Contains` and explicit "`r`n", and use `'(?m)^## Modes'` (no `$`) for the header check.
  - `Get-Content` strips line endings - never use it for the ending checks.
  - This task does NOT reopen Story S27 (see the Build order note): S27 stays `DONE`, and when T27.6 is ticked close-unit finds S27 already DONE and changes nothing in STORIES.md. QA runs upgrade-project ONLY on sandboxes under %TEMP% - never against the kit repo (D:\projects\DrDad) or D:\Tools.
  - Test pattern: `Test-Case "name" { ... Assert <bool> "message" }`. `Test-Case` itself sets `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"`; `Assert` throws at the first failure. ASCII only, PowerShell 5.1, no network.

### [x] T29.1 - install-mode.ps1: Resolve-InstallMode, the pure C6 mode resolver   (Story S29)
- **Goal:** a new dot-sourceable `install-mode.ps1` holds `Resolve-InstallMode`, which turns the three flags plus the existing `settings.json` into one mode, one reason and an optional conflict or warning. It writes nothing and prints nothing.
- **Touches:** `install-mode.ps1` (new, repo root). Nothing else.
- **Do:**
  1. Create `install-mode.ps1` (ASCII, PS 5.1, header comment citing C6). It defines only `function Resolve-InstallMode { param([bool]$LocalFlag, [bool]$CloudFlag, [bool]$HybridFlag, [string]$SettingsPath) ... }` - no top-level code.
  2. Return a `[pscustomobject]` with: `Mode` (`'Cloud'|'Local'|'Hybrid'`, or `$null` on conflict), `Reason` (`'default'|'kept'|'explicit'`), `ReasonText`, `Conflict` (string or `$null`), `Warn` (string or `$null`).
  3. Conflict first: `$LocalFlag` with `$CloudFlag` or `$HybridFlag` -> `Conflict` = a sentence naming BOTH switches, e.g. `-Local conflicts with -Hybrid` (when both others are set, name `-Cloud` and `-Hybrid`); `Mode` = `$null`; return.
  4. Explicit flag next: `$HybridFlag` -> Hybrid (also when `$CloudFlag` is set: Hybrid wins), else `$CloudFlag` -> Cloud, else `$LocalFlag` -> Local; `Reason` = `explicit`. Do NOT read the file in this case.
  5. No flag: read `$SettingsPath` if `Test-Path`. `ConvertFrom-Json` in `try`; a parse failure sets `Warn` = a sentence naming `$SettingsPath` and counts as nothing to keep -> Cloud, `default`. Parsed: `env.ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` present and non-empty -> Local, `kept`; else `env.LOCALTOOLS_HYBRID` equal to `1` (string compare, `"$v" -eq '1'`) -> Hybrid, `kept`; anything else (no file, no `env`, empty `env`, Cloud install) -> Cloud, `default`. Guard a missing `env` property (strict-mode-safe: use `.PSObject.Properties.Name -contains`).
  6. `ReasonText` per C6: `default` -> `default (no flag, nothing installed to keep)`; `kept` -> `kept from the existing install (pass -Cloud to switch)`; `explicit` -> `explicit flag`.
- **Acceptance:** with `. .\install-mode.ps1`: `(Resolve-InstallMode $false $false $false "<missing path>").Mode` is `Cloud` with `Reason` `default`; for a file `{ "env": { "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": "http://localhost:11434" } }` it is `Local`/`kept`; for `{ "env": { "LOCALTOOLS_HYBRID": "1" } }` it is `Hybrid`/`kept`; `-LocalFlag $true -HybridFlag $true` gives `Conflict` matching both `-Local` and `-Hybrid`. Calls write no file and print nothing.
- **Depends on:** T24.6 (queue position only: the Build order sequences T29 after the last open `test-kit.ps1` edit); no code dependency
- **Refs:** Story S29 Behavior + AC1/AC2; `docs/DESIGN.md` C6 "Switches", "Resolution, in this order" (steps 1-3), "The banner says why", worked examples rows 1-7, invariants (i)-(iv); R34. Style to copy: `harness-versions.ps1` (a dot-sourced functions-only file).
- **Context:** C6 pins this (rows: 1 no file -> Cloud/default; 2 base-URL -> Local/kept; 3 no base-URL + `LOCALTOOLS_HYBRID=1` -> Hybrid/kept; 4 Local state + `-Cloud` -> Cloud (explicit); 5 no file + `-Local` -> Local/explicit; 6 `-Local -Hybrid` -> conflict; 7 Cloud state (no base-URL, no hybrid flag) -> Cloud/default). The tells are the SAME ones R34 already uses (base-URL presence, `LOCALTOOLS_HYBRID`); no marker file. `-Cloud` with `-Hybrid` behaves as today (Hybrid wins the label). Counter-example that must not ship: row 1 giving Local, or row 2 giving Cloud. Pure function so tests can call it with a sandbox `$SettingsPath` and never touch the real `%USERPROFILE%\.claude` (R35b). No edit to `install.ps1` here (T29.2).

In the `$sandbox` branch: `.bashrc` is written under `$sandbox` (a file named `.bashrc` in that dir) instead of `$env:HOME`; USER `Path` and `DAD_HOME` are recorded into a plain file `$sandbox\machine-env.txt` (one `NAME=value` line each) instead of `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable`; every other item prints `  SKIP (sandbox): <what>` and does nothing.
  4. Reads stay as-is (reading the real environment is harmless). The `.claude` writes already follow `$env:USERPROFILE`; do not change them.
  5. The unset path: the original statement must sit unmodified in the `else` branch, so with `DAD_INSTALL_SANDBOX` unset the run does exactly what it did before.
- **Acceptance:** (a) source check: each machine-wide write named in step 2 appears only inside an `else` of an `if ($sandbox)` (or has a `SKIP (sandbox)` branch), and `git diff -w` on `install.ps1` shows no change to the original statements other than being nested in `else`; (b) behavior check in a sandbox only: `$env:USERPROFILE=<tmp>\home; $env:HOME=<tmp>\home; $env:DAD_INSTALL_SANDBOX=<tmp>\sb; powershell -File .\install.ps1 -Yes` leaves the real USER `Path`, `DAD_HOME` and the real `$env:HOME\.bashrc` unchanged and writes `<tmp>\sb\machine-env.txt`; (c) `test-kit.ps1 -SkipBuild` prints `0 failed`.
- **Depends on:** T29.2, T29.3
- **Refs:** Story S29 AC1/AC2 and Dev notes (R35b); `docs/DESIGN.md` C6 invariant (v); the `DAD_SMOKE_*` env-var test-hook style (e.g. `DAD_SMOKE_OLLAMA_SHOW`, T24.5). Code: install.ps1 step 7b and the lines after it, step 8, step 1 pulls, the harness-install blocks.
- **Context:** why: a real run writes the machine-wide USER PATH, `DAD_HOME` and `.bashrc` with no seam (R35b), so AC1/AC2 could only be tested through the pure resolver. The hook must be inert unless explicitly set, so a normal `install.cmd` is unaffected (the unset path is the acceptance). Do not touch the mode-resolution logic or banners. ASCII only, PowerShell 5.1. QA must run the behavior check only with all three env vars pointing under %TEMP%, never without `DAD_INSTALL_SANDBOX`.

### [x] T30.1 - ratchet.ps1: derive the test-marker set from FactAttribute/TheoryAttribute subclasses (transitive, fail-open) and use it in Find-ShrunkFiles   (Story S30)
- **Goal:** `[RealIpfsFact]`-style attributes declared anywhere in the project count as tests, in the total and in the per-file shrink report, and a discovery error falls back to the base markers with a note.
- **Touches:** `ratchet.ps1` only.
- **Do:**
  1. Keep `$testMarkers` (line ~69) as the BASE pattern. Directly after the `$srcFiles` scan (~line 76) add discovery over the `.cs`, `.fs`, `.vb` files in `$srcFiles` only, inside `try { ... } catch { $discoveryNote = "..."; $testMarkers = $baseMarkers }` (save `$baseMarkers = $testMarkers` first). Read each file with `Get-Content -Raw`.
  2. Discovery loop: `$derived = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.HashSet[string]`. Seed bases `FactAttribute`, `TheoryAttribute`. Repeat until a pass adds nothing: for each file, regex `\bclass\s+(\w+)\s*(?:<[^>]*>)?\s*:\s*([^{]+?)\s*(?:where\b|\{|$)` (multiline) gives class name + base list; split the base list on `,`; strip any namespace qualifier (text up to the last `.`); a base counts when it is in the current base set (seeds plus `$derived`). Add the class name to `$derived`. Because a derived name is also matched with its `Attribute` suffix removed/added, normalise: store the name without a trailing `Attribute`, and compare bases the same way (so `FactAttribute` and `Fact` are the same, `RealIpfsFact` and `RealIpfsFactAttribute` are the same).
  3. Comments and strings must not create a derived class: before matching, blank out `//` line comments, `/* */` blocks and `"..."` string literals in the text (replace with spaces). A line such as `// class Fake : FactAttribute` or `var s = "class Fake : FactAttribute";` adds nothing.
  4. Build the extra pattern: for each derived name `X` (excluding the seeds `Fact` and `Theory`, already in the base pattern), an alternative matching the attribute either alone or inside a list: `(?<=\[|,)\s*(?:X|XAttribute)\s*(?:\([^\]]*\))?\s*(?=\]|,)` (escape names with `[regex]::Escape`). Attribute lists such as `[Trait("a","b"), X]` and `[X(Skip="x")]` match; `[X, Trait(...)]` matches. Final `$testMarkers = $baseMarkers + '|' + (alternatives joined by '|')` when there is at least one derived name; unchanged otherwise.
  5. Counting stays per LINE (`Select-String -Pattern $testMarkers`, as today), so a line with two markers counts once. Do NOT change how the non-C# languages are counted.
  6. `Find-ShrunkFiles` (~line 131) already reads `$testMarkers` from the script scope for both the baseline content (`git show`) and the working-tree content; make sure it uses the final derived `$testMarkers` for BOTH (no second copy of the pattern). Also count per line there for consistency: `@(($text -split "`r?`n") | Where-Object { $_ -match $testMarkers }).Count` instead of `[regex]::Matches(...)` (a regex-wide Matches count and Select-String's per-line count can differ on a line with two markers).
  7. Fail-open note: when the discovery `catch` runs, print one line `ratchet: attribute discovery failed (<message>) - counting the base markers only` with Write-Host (not to the Json stream: under `-Json` use `Write-Host` too, and only before the JSON is emitted, as `Write-Host` does not pollute the object pipeline); the exit code and the rest of the run are unaffected. Provide the test seam: if the environment variable `DAD_RATCHET_FAIL_DISCOVERY` is set to `1`, throw inside the `try` so the fallback is testable (`Remove` of the seam is not needed; it is inert unset).
  8. Do not edit the baseline file format, `-Update`, or the drops logic.
- **Acceptance:** with a sandbox project holding `class RealIpfsFactAttribute : FactAttribute` and 10 methods marked `[RealIpfsFact]`, `ratchet.ps1 -ProjectDir <p> -Json` reports `current.tests` = 10 (and 10 with `[Fact]` instead; a baseline taken with `[Fact]` followed by the conversion gives no drop); `class Z : RealIpfsFactAttribute`, `class Q : Xunit.TheoryAttribute`, `[RealIpfsFactAttribute]`, `[RealIpfsFact(Skip="x")]` and `[Trait("a","b"), RealIpfsFact]` each count one test; a class that only mentions `FactAttribute` in a comment or string adds none; with `DAD_RATCHET_FAIL_DISCOVERY=1` the base markers are used, the note line is printed and the exit code is unchanged. Existing ratchet cases in `test-kit.ps1` still pass.
- **Depends on:** T29.7 (queue order only; no code dependency)
- **Refs:** Story S30 Behavior (bullets 1-4) and AC1-AC5; DESIGN R28 (fail open), R29. Code: `ratchet.ps1` `$testMarkers` (~69), the `$srcFiles` scan (~71-79), `Find-ShrunkFiles` (~131-151).
- **Context:** field report from `gdn1` (2026-10-09): `RealIpfsFactAttribute : FactAttribute` (and an internal `RealIpfsEnvFactAttribute : FactAttribute` declared in a test file, so discovery must scan every source file, not a shared one) moved the count 228 -> 219. Not in scope and must stay unchanged: `[Fact(Skip="...")]` and `[Fact, Trait(...)]` are NOT counted today (base markers are exact `[Fact]`); do not widen the base pattern. Derived-attribute alternatives however DO match with arguments and inside lists, as the story says. PowerShell 5.1, ASCII only. `$testMarkers` is also used by the non-C# alternatives in the same pattern (`def test_`, `it(`, ...); keep them.

When `$rCode -eq 0` and `$Reason` is non-empty, `$notes.Add("-Reason ignored: nothing shrank, so there was no shrink to accept")`. Each trailer stays its own `-m`; never a here-string.

  4. Run the full `.\test-kit.ps1`.
- **Acceptance:** full `.\test-kit.ps1` prints `0 failed` with the new case on a PASS line; the in-case mutation (one method removed) makes `recover-lost` exit 1 and name `Case6`.
- **Depends on:** T30.3 (anchor; sequential `test-kit.ps1` edit)
- **Refs:** Story S30 Behavior (last bullet) and AC7; `recover-lost.ps1` `Get-Units` (matches NAMES, attribute prefix ignored).
- **Context:** `recover-lost.ps1` params used by neighbours: `-ProjectDir`, `-Since <sha>`, `-Restore`; the clean message is `nothing named has vanished`. No behavior change to `recover-lost.ps1` is allowed in this story. ASCII only, PowerShell 5.1.

- **Goal:** prove the fix on the field-report repo without changing it, and record the change in `CHANGELOG.md`.

Snapshot gdn1 before: `git -C D:\projects\GalacticDataNetwork\gdn1 status --porcelain` and `git -C D:\projects\GalacticDataNetwork\gdn1 rev-parse HEAD`, plus the SHA/hash of `D:\projects\GalacticDataNetwork\gdn1\.claude\.dad-ratchet.json` (its baseline stays 219) and the line count of `grades\gates-log.jsonl`.

  3. Snapshot after: the `git status --porcelain` output may differ ONLY by the modified `grades/gates-log.jsonl` (the ratchet appends exactly one line via `Write-GateLog`); `HEAD` and the baseline file hash are unchanged and the gates-log gained exactly one line. Report this explicitly. Do not commit, restore or edit anything in gdn1; if anything else differs, STOP and report.
  4. `CHANGELOG.md`: add an entry at the top in the file's existing style, header `## 0.59.1 - 2026-10-09` (the next patch above the current `VERSION` 0.59.0; do NOT edit the `VERSION` file - the orchestrator releases), under Fixed/Changed: the ratchet counts tests marked by attributes derived from `FactAttribute`/`TheoryAttribute` (transitive, namespace-qualified, `Attribute` suffix, in attribute lists), fails open with a note, `Find-ShrunkFiles` uses the same set, `close-unit -Reason` records a `Shrink-accepted:` trailer with `-AcceptShrink`; mention S30 and the gdn1 228 vs 219 field report. Mention the non-change: `[Fact(Skip=...)]` is still not counted.

Running ratchet.ps1 without `-Update` and without a baseline mismatch exits 0 or 1 by comparison with 219 (228 > 219 is fine, exit 0). This is a build task, not a test-kit case: the suite must never touch gdn1. ASCII only.

## Open questions
- **[design] S30 follow-up (recorded, not changed):** `[Fact(Skip="...")]` and `[Fact, Trait(...)]` are not counted by the base markers (exact `[Fact]`); widening them would raise counts everywhere and re-baseline every project. Decide separately whether to widen.
- **RESOLVED (decided 2026-10-07; tasked as T29.8 and T29.9) [design] S29 AC1/AC2 end-to-end coverage.** The seam and the end-to-end cases are now tasks; the original note follows for history. **[design] S29 AC1/AC2 were verified through the pure resolver plus `dad-doctor`, not a full sandbox `install.ps1` run.** A real run of `install.ps1` writes the machine-wide USER `Path`, the `DAD_HOME` user variable and `$env:HOME\.bashrc` (step 7b and the lines after it) with no seam to redirect them, so an end-to-end "no flag -> Cloud settings.json" test would touch the real machine (R35b). T29.1/T29.5 pin the decision (C6 rows 1-7) in `Resolve-InstallMode`, T29.6 pins the read-back, and only the conflict case (which exits before any write) runs the script. If the human wants a true end-to-end install test, `install.ps1` needs a test seam (for example an env var that skips steps 7b/7c) - that is new scope and is not tasked.
- **RESOLVED (decided 2026-10-07: KEEP; T29.3 behavior and banner line) [design] S29 `-Cloud -Hybrid` and step 8.** Hybrid always runs the Ollama tuning. Original note: Today `-Cloud -Hybrid` skipped the Ollama tuning (`if (-not $Cloud)`); C6 says Hybrid wins the label and "Ollama ... and the step-8 GPU tuning are required for Local and Hybrid", so T29.3 tunes for Hybrid always. Amend T29.3 step 2 if the human wants the old behavior.
- **[design] S19 dad-doctor behaviour.** S19 lists `dad-doctor.ps1` but it only REPORTS the docs dir; T19.1 makes it WARN (and use the resolved dir for the RAG check) rather than silently choose a dir. If the human wants it to print the same `WARN: ignoring ...` line instead, amend T19.1 step 3.
- **[design] S18 file:line resolution rule is not pinned in DESIGN.** T18.1 uses: path relative to project root, else a unique same-name file under the project (excluding .git, _tmp, bin, obj, node_modules); the largest number of a `a-b` range must be <= line count. If the human wants a different rule (e.g. root-relative only), amend T18.1 before building.
- **[design] needs contract: the loop-guard writer's fallback if `PreToolUse` carries no `cwd`.** C3f's
  PREREQUISITE MEASUREMENT (sharded as T9.5) may come back NO. C3f deliberately does not pre-decide the
  fallback - "a machine-level log, or resolving the project another way - is a NEW decision to be taken
  then". No task is sharded for that fallback, and T9.2 must NOT improvise one: if T9.5 returns NO, this
  goes back to `/design` before any code is written.
- **[design] S27 `.gitattributes` matching subset.** S27 says a fresh file follows the project's `.gitattributes` `eol` "if one is declared for it" but does not say how patterns match. T27.1 pins a small subset: patterns `*`, the exact file name, and `*.<ext>`; `eol=lf`/`eol=crlf` only; last applying line wins; directory patterns, negation and macros ignored; a file with no newline uses this fresh-file rule. If the human wants full gitattributes semantics (or `git check-attr`), amend T27.1 step 1 before building.
- Story S3's AC4 ("(manual) `/scaffold avalonia` appears in the menu and scaffolds a project") is a manual,
  human-run verification step, not a shardable dev task - it was confirmed at implementation time per the
  story's note ("AC4 (manual /scaffold avalonia) confirm on the 5080") and is not represented as a separate
  task here.
- Story S11 ("[SPIKE] Should high-stakes verification run as a separate process?") is deliberately NOT
  sharded into any task here. It is a research/decision spike, not a build story - its own deliverable (a
  written recommendation weighing R32's same-session subagent discipline against claude-code-audit-gate's

  under "Recommendation (draft - pending human decision, satisfies AC1)". There is no code to shard: S11's
  own text says "no build work follows from this story until a human decision is made" among options A
  (stay same-session), B (fully separate-process), or C (hybrid). `doc-stats -Findings`'s `[taskmap]`
  finding will keep reporting S11 as having no tasks - that is correct and expected, not a gap to fill,
  until a human picks an option and a follow-up story/task is scoped for whichever one is chosen.

- **RESOLVED 2026-10-01 - [design] S23 count definition vs the confirmation line itself.** S23 now
  excludes the confirmation line itself from the count (matching T23.1 step 2 and T23.2 AC2 as written).
  Original note follows. The required line
  `Security waiver confirmed: <date> (human; auth-keyword hits: <N>)` contains `auth-keyword`, which matches
  `\bauth\b`. Counted literally, adding the printed line raises M by one, so M > N and the WARN re-fires
  forever, and AC2's "N=3 and 3 hits" fixture would read 4. T23.1 therefore EXCLUDES the confirmation line
  from M (every other match in the design doc and STORIES.md is counted, as S23 Behavior 2 says). If the
  human prefers a literal count, amend T23.1 step 2 and the AC2 expectation in T23.2 before building.

  and the dotnet / ASP.NET-framework guards in the api-surface case - still count as PASS when they run

- **[design] after T24.6: C4a's "DONE 2026-10-03 via S24" text goes stale (not blocking).** That paragraph
  says "The S24 code still prints `unknown` for `<ctx>`; applying the 2026-10-03 source rule above is not yet
  implemented." Once T24.6 closes this is no longer true, and neither task may edit DESIGN.md, so a `/design`
  pass should record it as done. Also unpinned by DESIGN, and settled only inside T24.5: a value is accepted
  when its text is all digits (so a JSON string `"40960"` counts, a float `40960.0` is `unknown`); the first
  `num_ctx` line wins; and a forced `DAD_SMOKE_OLLAMA=up` does not suppress the real `/api/show` call (only
  `DAD_SMOKE_OLLAMA_SHOW` does). Amend T24.5 before building if the human wants any of these otherwise.
